@@129740251 <p> I find it frustrating to truly grasp the immense pain and suffering in the Gaza Strip . For most of us , it retreats into the distance -- just old news stories , forgotten headlines , or cold , impersonal statistics . <p> But we can not let it stay there . When we look past the political posturing and the complicated legal language , a massive tragedy is still unfolding , leaving a deep , terrible mark on millions of lives . The initial shock of the conflict has long gone , replaced by a relentless , grinding campaign of destruction , hunger , and mass displacement . This is not a story from the past ; it is an ongoing human catastrophe that demands the world 's immediate , unwavering focus . This week , I look beyond the numbers and try to understand what that reality truly means . <p> The war in Gaza , which escalated dramatically in late 2023 and has continued unabated into 2025 , has been characterized by an unprecedented scale of civilian casualties and infrastructure destruction . As of September 2025 @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ killed and more than 167,000 injured , with women and children consistently making up the vast majority of those killed . This proportion -- estimated by independent sources and even internal Israeli intelligence to be over 80% civilian -- is one of the highest recorded in modern conflict , leading to widespread accusations of war crimes and acts of genocide . <p> The destruction has been systematic , rendering entire swaths of the enclave uninhabitable . Over 90% of homes have been damaged or destroyed , and the core infrastructure of civil society has been shattered . <p> The catastrophic collapse of Gaza 's healthcare system , where over 94% of hospitals have been damaged or destroyed , has forced the few remaining facilities to operate at nearly 300% capacity amid a critical scarcity of life-saving supplies like insulin and antibiotics . Compounding this crisis is the decimation of education , with virtually all 12 universities and over 97% of schools damaged or destroyed , an act that robs an entire generation of its future . Beyond the physical and immediate humanitarian suffering , this destruction extends to the deliberate @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ heritage sites have been reduced to rubble , systematically dismantling the very foundations of society . <p> The policy of destruction extends beyond bombardment to include forcible displacement . Nearly 85% of Gaza 's population , over 1.9 million people , have been repeatedly forced to flee , often directed into areas , like the coastal stretch of al-Mawasi , that were supposedly designated as humanitarian safe zones but have become the new front lines . <p> Perhaps the most harrowing aspect of the ongoing catastrophe is the deliberate creation of a famine-level humanitarian crisis . By tightening the blockade and restricting aid , food , water , and medicine have been instrumentalized as tools of war . This weaponization of essential supplies is confirmed by data showing that , as of late 2025 , 100% of the population faces high levels of acute food insecurity , with hundreds of thousands experiencing catastrophic levels . Consequently , malnutrition rates among young children have soared , resulting in hundreds of confirmed starvation-related deaths , while the price of necessities has become utterly unaffordable . <p> The supposed humanitarian zone of al-Mawasi @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ seen its population swell to over 425,000 people living in hell-like makeshift tents . Despite its protected status , it has been subjected to relentless attacks -- including air , drone , and sniper fire -- that have killed and wounded hundreds of civilians , shattering any illusion of safety . The initial attacks , such as the widely reported strikes on or near the camp in May and June 2024 , highlight a grim reality : no place in Gaza is safe . <p> The peril for civilians is compounded by the fact that the very act of seeking aid has become deadly . <p> Thousands of Palestinians have been killed or injured while trying to reach supply points or aid convoys . <p> Moreover , the number of humanitarian aid workers killed in Gaza -- over 540 since October 2023 -- is the highest recorded in any single conflict in history , effectively paralyzing the relief effort . <p> The sheer volume of human suffering and the systematic nature of destruction represent a profound failure of international protection . Incidents , whether deemed " operational errors " or @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ disregard for civilian life that violates the core tenets of international law . <p> In the face of such calculated devastation , we can not claim ignorance ; this moment is the ultimate crucible for our collective humanity . To remain silent , to be a passive observer , is to be an accomplice to atrocity . <p> The defense of human dignity now falls squarely on all of us -- to amplify the work of those brave souls documenting the truth , to demand accountability from the halls of power to the courtrooms of international justice , and to relentlessly pierce the manufactured narratives that seek to obscure this suffering . The people of Gaza are not abstractions : they are the children enduring an amputation without anesthesia , the family repeatedly displaced , the elderly dying from preventable starvation . Their individual cries for justice must become a global roar that shatters the indifference of the world . The wounds inflicted here will scar generations , but our immediate , non-negotiable moral duty is to scream for mercy until it arrives , to fight for justice until it @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ away from the bloodstain this catastrophe is leaving on our collective soul .

@@129786705 <h> Related News <p> IT is getting harder every day to do the fundamental journalistic task of collecting facts and verifying information . It is also getting much more dangerous . <p> In the first half of 2025 , the climate facing journalists darkened to levels not seen in decades , reflecting a coordinated escalation of violence , intimidation , and censorship . <p> The fabric of societies is being torn apart by what in many cases are deliberate and calculated campaigns to undermine facts , the bedrock of our shared reality . There is no need to prove things are untrue , you only need to relentlessly smear , sow doubt and float conspiracies . Often social media algorithms will do the rest . <p> For Agence France-Presse ( AFP ) , an international news agency with a reporting team spread across the globe , the figures are stark : we had 25 serious incidents involving journalists working for us in the first six months of this year . That is more than occurred in the whole of 2024 . <p> But these incidents of assaults , arrests , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ the full scale of the global assault on the public 's right to information . <p> The geographical spread of violence and intimidation is widening . The situation is aggravated by the rise of authoritarian practices and populist rhetoric that openly targets the press . Law enforcement 's growing impunity -- emboldened by prevailing political messages -- has made physical assaults on journalists commonplace . <p> This is not a phenomenon isolated to so-called unstable regimes ; it is surfacing in established democracies and countries with long traditions of press freedom , pointing to a dangerous shift in global norms . <p> Journalists have traditionally identified themselves at protests and public events , believing this identity carried some form of protection and legitimacy . But we increasingly see these identifiers as targets . <p> Over the past year journalists working for us have been targeted in different and violent ways at protests in countries as varied as Turkey , Argentina and the United States . All of them were clearly identified as press . All of them are convinced they were targeted because they were journalists . <p> In significant @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ The intimidation and threats have become unmanageable . <p> We have seen journalists working for us being forced to flee from across the Sahel area of West Africa and also in areas of Central America such as Nicaragua and El Salvador . <p> In parts of Eastern and Central Europe our fact-checkers face regular death threats and campaigns to intimidate and silence . <p> The message often comes from the top . The presidency in Argentina posted on social media last year : " We Do Not Hate Journalists Enough " . <p> In total the Argentine Journalism Forum recorded 179 assaults on media workers in 2024 . <p> And then there is Gaza . The Committee to Protect Journalists ( CPJ ) says nearly 200 journalists in Gaza have been killed over the past two years . <p> It points to over 20 incidents where it believes individuals were deliberately targeted . <p> Some of the journalists who work for AFP in Gaza refuse to wear their protective flak jackets because they fear it will make them a target . They also say people are scared to be near @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ yet perhaps what is most notable is how few governments in the many countries that have thrived on press freedom are prepared to raise their heads above the parapet to stand up for facts , truth and press freedom . Many brave and dedicated journalists feel horribly alone at this point . <p> This assault on journalism and campaign to undermine facts comes as the management of our digital lives is increasingly ? transferring to powerful Generative Artificial Intelligence tools . <p> We can all see the astonishing ? capacities these tools have for knowledge building and human advancement , but we are also already seeing how they can be used to pollute our information ecosystem with vast amounts of false and made-up content . <p> This feels like an inflection point . People talk casually about living in a post-truth world . Journalism is imperfect , it will not always get it right . But the honest aspiration to gather information and seek the truth is fundamental to the healthy functioning of our societies . <p> Now , more than ever , we need to stand up for facts @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is Global News Director at Agence France-Presse . These articles were commissioned as part of World News Day -- a global campaign celebrated on Sept 28 every year to highlight the value of journalism . It is organised by WAN-IFRA 's World Editors Forum , Project Kontinuum and The Canadian Journalism Foundation . 
@@129786706 <h> #WND2025 : You deserve the truth , not AI 's interpretation of it <p> WE all want to understand the world around us . Perhaps we want more clarity about the war in Gaza , or what our government is doing about the healthcare our family relies on . It might be something as simple as changes to a bus route that will affect our daily commute . No matter how momentous or mundane the issue , we have a right to news we can trust . <p> We 've all been there , scrolling our feed , seeing an astonishing clip , or a shocking must-share story . But now we must constantly question what 's real and what 's the creation of artificial intelligence . <p> AI-generated output is so convincing today and shaping so much of the information we ? consume that we risk being unable to trust anything anymore . And mistrust is the fuel that drives conspiracism , social polarisation and democratic disengagement . <p> Liz CorbinIn reality , the integrity of what we call ' news ' is being eroded by the tools @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ world . <p> This World News Day , we want to underline that the public are entitled to the facts that professional journalists and the ? organisations they work for worldwide are committed to ? finding , corroborating and sharing . However , the tech companies building AI systems that millions of people use daily are falling far short of their responsibility to truth . <p> Original research carried out this year by the BBC found that half of AI-generated answers to news-related requests left out important details and made other key errors . <p> So what ? It 's useful and saves time , it 'll improve , we can live with the errors . Except we 're not talking about a cake recipe or holiday recommendations . Democracy is at stake , because a society with no common understanding of what 's true ca n't make informed choices . And individuals who rely on a deceptive distortion of originally independent , accurate journalism risk losing themselves in a toxic mire of half-truths and bad-faith manipulation . <p> This is n't faraway , abstract paranoia . The internet is @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ clicks , and promote vested interests . AI-generated voices , faces , and headlines are degrading the information ecosystem , often with no clear provenance or accountability . <p> Meanwhile , the output of journalists serving the public interest , especially in local , regional , and independent media , is being scraped without permission , algorithmically repackaged and redistributed with no credit or compensation . <p> This phenomenon is arguably more pernicious than the glaring , outrageous deepfakes we have all seen because the inaccuracies are subtle , plausible and more likely to mislead . We are witnessing the sabotage of news we need to be able to rely on , and that is draining already depleted reserves of public trust . <p> So , what can be done ? <p> The European Broadcasting Union and WAN-IFRA , together with a fast-growing collective of other organisations representing thousands of professional journalists and newsrooms around the world , are calling for urgent changes to how AI developers interact with news and the people who produce it . <p> Many of the broadcasters and news publishers we represent are using AI @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ such as through automating translation , helping detect misinformation , or personalising content . They are mindful that the deployment of these tools must be principled , transparent and carefully handled . <p> That 's why we are presenting five clear requirements to AI tech companies . These are not radical ; they are realistic , common-sense standards that any ethical technology developer can and should embrace : <p> 1 . No content without consent . AI systems must not be trained on news content without permission . That content is intellectual property created through rigorous work and public trust . Unsanctioned scraping is theft that undermines both . <p> 2 . Respect value . High-quality journalism is expensive to ? produce but vital for ? society 's wellbeing . AI tools ? benefiting from that work must compensate its ? creators fairly and in good faith . <p> 3 . Be transparent . When AI-generated content feeds on news sources , those sources should always be clearly cited and linked because accuracy and attribution matter . We are entitled to know where information came from and if it differs @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ . We invite AI companies to enter a serious , solutions-driven dialogue with the news industry . Together , we can develop standards for accuracy , safety , and transparency , but only if tech companies see journalists as partners , and not as suppliers of free data to be mined and monetised . <p> We consider this a civic challenge that affects every person who relies on credible information to make decisions about their life , to form credible opinions or decide whom to vote for . <p> Tech companies talk a lot about trust , but trust is not built on talk . We 're calling on the leaders of the AI revolution to get a handle on this problem now . They have the power to shape the future of information , but we do n't yet see them taking their tools ' dangerous shortcomings , and the potential consequences of them , seriously enough . <p> Without urgent , corrective action , AI wo n't just distort the news -- it will destroy the public 's ability to trust in anything and anyone , which will @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ is Director of News at EBU while Vincent Peyr ? gne is CEO of WAN-IFRA.

@@129864213 <p> On 12 September 2025 , the United Nations General Assembly ( UNGA ) overwhelmingly voted to endorse the New York Declaration , which was issued in July after an international conference hosted by Saudi Arabia and France . <p> The most important aspect of the declaration is the so-called Two-State Solution Roadmap . The sponsors claim that the declaration lays out a single roadmap toward a viable and sovereign State of Palestine co-existing with Israel , with a focus on tangible , time-bound , and irreversible steps . They also called on the international community to make progress on statehood , governance , security , and recognition of Palestine in a phased and monitored manner . <p> The UN General Assembly votes on whether to back the " New York Declaration , " a resolution that seeks to breathe new life into the two-state solution between Israel and the Palestinians . They were very clear from @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ a colonial project but a settler-colonial project . <p> The project , therefore , requires the demographics of the state to be such that the population is entirely or predominantly Jewish . As a result , the Zionist leaders had always perceived the indigenous Palestinian population to be a significant obstacle to the project . They were seen as aliens ' who must eventually be removed from the land . <p> Jewish immigrants arriving in Port Haifa , 1947 . <p> It has to be borne in mind that in 1947 , Jews constituted only one-third of the population of Palestine . Moreover , most of them were Europeans who had arrived in Palestine only three years prior . The relatively smaller number of Jews compared to Palestinians , therefore , was a problem that needed to be solved . <p> The Western powers colluded with the project by using the United Nations , which came up with a partition plan where 55 per cent of the area was allocated to the Jewish newcomers and 42 per cent to the indigenous Arabs . The plan 's objective was obviously to @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ they constituted only one-third of the population . It also served to protect the image of the Jews as being interested in implementing a democratic system of governance there . The Arabs , understandably , rejected the plan . This led to the Arab-Israeli War in 1948 , during which Zionist paramilitary gangs attacked Palestinian villages , killing thousands and driving the rest , about 750,000 , out to become refugees . When the war ended , the Zionists managed to control 80 per cent of the land . <p> The UN Partition Plan of 1947 ( UN General Assembly Resolution 181 ) proposed to divide British-ruled Mandatory Palestine into two independent states , one Jewish and one Arab , with Jerusalem under international control . <p> But the Zionists were very clear that their project to set up the Zionist state , which stretches from the Jordan River to the Mediterranean Sea , was not yet complete . The Zionists wanted the land but not the people , and they could not possibly expel them immediately from the land . <p> The solution was to keep the Arabs under continuous occupation . In this way , Palestinians can never obtain their rights as citizens of Israel , thereby preserving the favourable demographics . For the Zionists , Arabs are allowed to exist , but their existence in the West Bank and Gaza is in the form of a lesser human being , confined to some Bantustans or enclaves . <p> In the meantime , Israel continues to expand the number of settlements in the West Bank , which has been the policy of all the governments of Israel , whether they were left-leaning or nationalist . If Rabin was serious about letting Palestinians control the West Bank under the so-called two-state solution , why the heck did he bother to spend so much money on the new roads ? <p> The fact of the matter is , after the Oslo Accords , Israel has not made any serious offer that the Palestinians can agree to . The Palestinian state ' , which was offered to them , does not possess contiguous lands . Moreover , under the terms of the offer , Israel was supposed to be able to keep @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ encircled East Jerusalem , cutting it off from the rest of the West Bank . Moreover , these settlements are located on water aquifers , thereby denying the Palestinians control over the water resources on the land that was supposed to be their state . Finally , Israel wanted complete control over the new state 's airspace and borders . <p> In the meantime , the international community has not taken any significant action over the last five decades to make the two-state option a reality , not even after the Oslo Accord was signed in 1993 . @@129864524 <p> PETALING JAYA : The invitation extended to US President Donald Trump for the upcoming 47th Asean Summit should be seen as part of Malaysia 's responsibility as Asean Chair , not as an endorsement of any leader 's policies , said the Prime Minister 's Office ( PMO ) . <p> Senior Press Secretary to the Prime Minister Tunku Nashrul Tunku Abaidah said Malaysia , as chair , is tasked with ensuring the presence of all dialogue partners , adding that the country must seize the opportunity to showcase leadership on the global stage . <p> " Rejecting Trump 's attendance is not the solution . Diplomacy requires us to speak the truth directly , not avoid it . <p> " Malaysia will continue to stand firm on Palestine , while also fulfilling its responsibility to lead ASEAN in building a prosperous and stable region , " he said at the PMO briefing in Putrajaya today . <p> The PMO reaffirmed Malaysia 's unwavering position on Palestine , stressing that Prime Minister Datuk Seri Anwar Ibrahim has consistently condemned Israeli aggression and has been one of the most vocal @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ speak with one voice and work with all partners -- not only the US , but also China , Japan , Korea , Australia and the Gulf Cooperation Council ( GCC ) , " said Tunku Nashrul . <p> The 47th Asean Summit will take place in Kuala Lumpur this October , marking the last time Malaysia holds the chairmanship before handing it over to the Philippines . <p> On humanitarian efforts , the PMO noted Anwar 's role as patron of Sumud Nusantara , a Malaysian initiative delivering aid to Gaza . <p> " The Prime Minister is closely monitoring the situation of local activists now operating in high-risk zones , while urging for humanitarian corridors to remain open . <p> " Their courage reflects the compassion of Malaysians for the suffering in Gaza , " he said . <p> Meanwhile , the PMO also said starting today , all 16 million eligible Malaysians can purchase RON95 petrol at RM1.99 per litre , compared to the unsubsidised price of RM2.60 per litre . <p> Since its launch over the weekend for members of the Armed Forces , police , @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ @ than 912,000 people have already redeemed Budi95 benefits , amounting to 21.8 million litres of subsidised fuel worth RM43.7 million . <p> " Each eligible individual is entitled to 300 litres a month , equivalent to a subsidy of up to RM183 . <p> " The government is also working with e-hailing operators to provide additional quotas for drivers , and is studying ways to extend subsidies to boat operators in Sabah and Sarawak . <p> " The Madani government will continue to strengthen this policy to ease the burden on the people , " Tunku Nashrul added .
